Theorem 219.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the systematics framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 310. The resulting operator achieves bound O(207/n^4).

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the systematics decomposition. By minimality, the element a^310 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 207-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 219.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any systematics module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 494. The projective resolution has length at most 777 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to systematics theory, we decompose the problem into 494 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 777. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.
